{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{}{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lxSVG@setdash{3.0pt,3.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 20.7 20.37 L 34.42 34.75}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {{}}{}{{}}
{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{}{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lxSVG@setdash{3.0pt,3.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 20.7 -20.37 L 34.42 -34.75}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {{}}{}{{}}
{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{}{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lxSVG@setdash{3.0pt,3.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 75.85 -34.78 L 92.65 -17.62}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {{}}{}{{}}
{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{}{{{{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lxSVG@setdash{3.0pt,3.0pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 75.85 34.78 L 92.65 17.62}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}~~=\big[\mathbf{B}^{3}\big]_{mn}.

(280)

Hence, only the diagrams
\textbf{V},~\textbf{VI}
and
VII
contribute the integral in Eq. (
271
).

We now compute the degeneracy of the aforementioned diagrams. For the diagram
V
, one hand of any
\mathbf{B}
can be contracted with one hand of another
\mathbf{B}
in
2\times 2=4
ways. For each of these cases, the third
\mathbf{B}
can be contracted with the open hands of the first two
\mathbf{B}
’s in
2
ways. Hence, the total degeneracy of the diagram is
8
.

For diagram
\mathbf{VI}
, which among the three
\mathbf{B}
’s will be contracted with the free indices
m
and
n
can be chosen in
3
ways. Upon choosing the
\mathbf{B}
in one of these ways, which of its two hands will be connected with the free indices
m
and
n
results in a degeneracy factor of
2
. The rest of the two
\mathbf{B}
’s can be contracted among themselves in
2
ways. Hence, the total degeneracy of the diagram is
12
.

For diagram
\mathbf{VII}
, which among the three
\mathbf{B}
’s will be contracted with the free indices
m
can be chosen in
3
ways. Upon choosing it, which among the rest two
\mathbf{B}
’s will be contracted with the free indices
n
can be chosen in
2
ways. Upon choosing the
\mathbf{B}
’s, in each case, we have two choices about which hand to be contracted with the free indices,
m
or
n
. After doing this, the free hands of these two
\mathbf{B}
will be contracted with the third
\mathbf{B}
, and it can be done in
2
ways. Hence, the total degeneracy of the diagram is
3\times 2\times 2\times 2\times 2=48
.

Combining everything, we obtain the value of the integral in Eq. (
271
) as

\displaystyle\int\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\otimes\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\Big(\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\cdot\mathbf{B}\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\Big)^{3}d\Omega=\frac{4\Omega_{D}}{D(D+2)(D+4)(D+6)}

\displaystyle\times\bigg[2\mathrm{Tr}\big[\mathbf{B}^{3}\big]\mathbf{I}_{D}+3\mathrm{Tr}\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]\mathbf{B}+12\mathbf{B}^{3}\bigg].

(281)

Appendix M
Derivation of Eq. (
68
)

Using the definition of
\big(\mathbb{T}^{(n)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\otimes n}\big)
from Eq. (
64
), we may write the
km
-th component of the integral
\int\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\otimes\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\big(\mathbb{T}^{(n)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\otimes n}\big)d\Omega
as

\displaystyle\bigg[\int\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\otimes\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(n)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\otimes n}\Big)d\Omega\bigg]_{km}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{D}\cdots\sum_{i_{n}=1}^{D}T^{(n)}_{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{n}}\int\sigma_{k}\sigma_{m}\sigma_{i_{1}}\sigma_{i_{2}}\ldots\sigma_{i_{n}}d\Omega.

(282)

If
n
is odd, then the integral contains the product of odd number of
\sigma
’s. Hence, using Eq. (
246
), we immediately get
\int\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\otimes\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(n)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\otimes n}\Big)d\Omega=0
when
n
is odd. For even
n
and
n\geq 4
, following a similar argument as given in Appendix
J
, we may show
\int\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\otimes\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(n)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\otimes n}\Big)d\Omega=0
. The only case left to compute is
n=2
. Focusing on the
km
-th component and using Eq. (
246
), we obtain

\displaystyle\bigg[\int\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\otimes\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(2)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\otimes\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\Big)d\Omega\bigg]_{km}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{D}T^{(2)}_{i_{1},i_{2}}\int\sigma_{k}\sigma_{m}\sigma_{i_{1}}\sigma_{i_{2}}d\Omega=\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{D}T^{(2)}_{i_{1},i_{2}}

\displaystyle\times\bigg[\frac{\Omega_{D}}{D(D+2)}\bigg(\delta_{i_{1}i_{2}}\delta_{mk}+\delta_{i_{1}m}\delta_{i_{2}k}+\delta_{i_{1}k}\delta_{i_{2}m}\bigg)\bigg].

(283)

Using the traceless property of
\mathbb{T}^{(2)}
, we have
\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{D}T^{(2)}_{i_{1},i_{2}}\delta_{i_{1}i_{2}}=0
. Using the symmetric property of
\mathbb{T}^{(2)}
, we further obtain
\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{D}T^{(2)}_{i_{1},i_{2}}\delta_{i_{1}m}\delta_{i_{2}k}=\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{D}T^{(2)}_{i_{1},i_{2}}\delta_{i_{1}k}\delta_{i_{2}m}=T^{(2)}_{km}
. Using these results back into Eq. (
283
), we obtain

\bigg[\int\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\otimes\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(2)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\otimes\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\Big)d\Omega\bigg]_{km}=\frac{2\Omega_{D}}{D(D+2)}T^{(2)}_{km},

(284)

which immediately gives

\displaystyle\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\cdot\bigg[\int\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\prime}\otimes\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\prime}\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(2)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\prime}\otimes\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\prime}\Big)d\Omega^{\prime}\bigg]\vec{\bm{\sigma}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\Omega_{D}}{D(D+2)}\sum_{k=1}^{D}\sum_{m=1}^{D}T^{(2)}_{km}\sigma_{k}\sigma_{m}=\frac{2\Omega_{D}}{D(D+2)}\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(2)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\otimes\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\Big).

(285)

Combining everything, we thus obtain

\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\cdot\bigg[\int\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\prime}\otimes\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\prime}\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(n)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\prime\otimes n}\Big)d\Omega^{\prime}\bigg]\vec{\bm{\sigma}}=\frac{\delta_{n,2}2\Omega_{D}}{D(D+2)}\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(n)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\otimes n}\Big).

(286)

Equation (
286
) is the desired Eq. (
68
) of the main text.

Appendix N
Checking of Eq. (
115
)

Here we show that the expression of the elements of
\mathbb{T}^{(4)}
as given in Eq. (
115
) is indeed traceless and symmetric. Since
b_{4}\Big(\mathrm{Tr}\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big],\mathrm{Tr}\big[\mathbf{B}^{3}\big],\ldots,\mathrm{Tr}\big[\mathbf{B}^{D}\big]\Big)
is simply a scalar, we will represent it as
b_{4}
in this section. Hence, Eq. (
115
) becomes

\displaystyle T^{(4)}_{ijkl}=b_{4}\Bigg[\Big(B_{ij}B_{kl}+B_{ik}B_{jl}+B_{il}B_{jk}\Big)

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{(D+2)}\Big[\delta_{ij}\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]_{kl}+\delta_{ik}\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]_{jl}+\delta_{il}\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]_{jk}

\displaystyle+\delta_{jk}\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]_{il}+\delta_{jl}\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]_{ik}+\delta_{kl}\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]_{ij}\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{2\mathrm{Tr}\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]}{(D+2)(D+4)}\Big(\delta_{ij}\delta_{kl}+\delta_{ik}\delta_{jl}+\delta_{il}\delta_{jk}\Big)\Bigg].

(287)

Since
\mathbf{B}
is a symmetric matrix, we have
B_{ab}=B_{ba}
and
\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]_{ab}=\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]_{ba}
. Further, from the definition, we have
\delta_{ab}=\delta_{ba}
. Using these properties, we immediately see that
T^{(4)}_{ijkl}
remains invariant under interchange of any pair of its indices, i.e.,
i\leftrightarrow j
or
i\leftrightarrow k
or
i\leftrightarrow l
or
j\leftrightarrow k
or
j\leftrightarrow l
or
k\leftrightarrow l
. Hence,
\mathbb{T}^{(4)}
is a symmetric tensor.

Since
\mathbb{T}^{(4)}
is symmetric, vanishing of the trace over any one pair of indices implies vanishing of all traces, indicating tracelessness of
\mathbb{T}^{(4)}
. In the following, we evaluate the trace by contracting the
ij
index pair. By definition, the trace is

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{j=1}^{D}\delta_{ij}\mathbb{T}^{(4)}_{ijkl}

\displaystyle=b_{4}\Bigg[\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{D}B_{ii}\Big)B_{kl}+\sum_{i=1}^{D}B_{ik}B_{il}+\sum_{i=1}^{D}B_{il}B_{ik}

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{(D+4)}\Big[(D+2)\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]_{kl}+2\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]_{lk}+\delta_{kl}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{D}\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]_{ii}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{2\mathrm{Tr}\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]}{(D+2)(D+4)}\Big(D\delta_{kl}+2\sum_{i=1}^{D}\delta_{il}\delta_{ik}\Big)\Bigg].

(288)

Since
\mathbf{B}
is traceless, we have
\sum_{i=1}^{D}B_{ii}=0
. The symmetric property of
\mathbf{B}
gives
\sum_{i=1}^{D}B_{ik}B_{il}=\sum_{i=1}^{D}B_{il}B_{ik}=\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]_{kl}
. The symmetric property of
\mathbf{B}^{2}
gives
\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]_{kl}=\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]_{lk}
. Further, from the definition, we have
\sum_{i=1}^{D}\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]_{ii}=\mathrm{Tr}\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]
and
\sum_{i=1}^{D}\delta_{il}\delta_{ik}=\delta_{kl}
. Hence, Eq. (
288
) simplifies to

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{D}\sum_{j=1}^{D}\delta_{ij}\mathbb{T}^{(4)}_{ijkl}

\displaystyle=b_{4}\Bigg[2\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]_{kl}-\frac{2}{(D+4)}\Big[(D+4)\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]_{kl}

\displaystyle+\mathrm{Tr}\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]\delta_{kl}\Big]+\frac{2\mathrm{Tr}\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]}{(D+4)}\delta_{kl}\Bigg]=0.

(289)

We have thus proved that
\mathbb{T}^{(4)}
is traceless and symmetric.

Appendix O
Derivation of
\vec{\nabla}_{\mathscr{S}}\big(\mathbb{T}^{(4)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\otimes 4}\big)

Similar to Appendix
I
, we start by defining

\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(4)},\vec{\mathbf{r}}^{\otimes 4}\Big)\equiv\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{3}=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{4}=1}^{D}T^{(n)}_{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4}}x_{i_{1}}x_{i_{2}}x_{i_{3}}x_{i_{4}},

(290)

where
\vec{\mathbf{r}}=r\vec{\bm{\sigma}}=(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{D})
, which immediately gives

\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(4)},\vec{\mathbf{r}}^{\otimes 4}\Big)=r^{4}\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(4)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\otimes 4}\Big).

(291)

Computing the
k
-th component of
\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(4)},\vec{\mathbf{r}}^{\otimes 4}\Big)
in the Cartesian coordinates, we obtain using Eq. (
236
) that

\left[\vec{\nabla}\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(4)},\vec{\mathbf{r}}^{\otimes 4}\Big)\right]_{k}=4\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{3}=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{4}=1}^{D}T^{(4)}_{k,i_{2},i_{3},i_{4}}x_{i_{2}}x_{i_{3}}x_{i_{4}},

(292)

where
\vec{\nabla}
is the full gradient operator. The right side of Eq. (
292
) can be thought as the
k
-th Cartesian component of the vector obtained when a rank-
4
tensor
\mathbb{T}^{(4)}
is contracted with
\vec{\mathbf{r}}\otimes\vec{\mathbf{r}}\otimes\vec{\mathbf{r}}
. In this notation, we may write

\vec{\nabla}\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(4)},\vec{\mathbf{r}}^{\otimes 4}\Big)=4\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(4)},\vec{\mathbf{r}}^{\otimes 3}\Big)=4r^{3}\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(4)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\otimes 3}\Big).

(293)

We now compute
\vec{\nabla}\big(\mathbb{T}^{(4)},\vec{\mathbf{r}}^{\otimes 4}\big)
in
D
-dimensional spherical-polar coordinates as defined in Appendix
B
. Using Eq. (
199
) along with Eq. (
291
) and noting the fact that
\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(4)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\otimes 4}\Big)
is independent of
r
, we obtain

\displaystyle\vec{\nabla}\big(\mathbb{T}^{(4)},\vec{\mathbf{r}}^{\otimes 4}\big)=\bigg[\hat{\mathbf{r}}\frac{\partial}{\partial r}+\frac{1}{r}\vec{\nabla}_{\mathscr{S}}\bigg]\bigg[r^{4}\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(4)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\otimes 4}\Big)\bigg]

\displaystyle=4r^{3}\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(4)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\otimes 4}\Big)\hat{\mathbf{r}}+r^{3}\vec{\nabla}_{\mathscr{S}}\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(4)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\otimes 4}\Big).

(294)

Note that, by definition
\hat{\mathbf{r}}=\vec{\mathbf{r}}/r=\vec{\bm{\sigma}}
, using which we may write

\vec{\nabla}\big(\mathbb{T}^{(4)},\vec{\mathbf{r}}^{\otimes 4}\big)=4r^{3}\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(4)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\otimes 4}\Big)\vec{\bm{\sigma}}+r^{3}\vec{\nabla}_{\mathscr{S}}\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(4)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\otimes 4}\Big).

(295)

Comparing Eqs. (
293
) and (
295
), we obtain

\vec{\nabla}_{\mathscr{S}}\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(4)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\otimes 4}\Big)=4\Big[\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(4)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\otimes 3}\Big)-\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(4)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\otimes 4}\Big)\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\Big].

(296)

We now express the right hand side of Eq. (
296
) in terms of
\mathbf{B}
and
\vec{\bm{\sigma}}
using Eq. (
115
). We will denote
b_{4}\Big(\mathrm{Tr}\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big],\mathrm{Tr}\big[\mathbf{B}^{3}\big],\ldots,\mathrm{Tr}\big[\mathbf{B}^{D}\big]\Big)
by
b_{4}
in the following computation. Focusing on the
k
-th component of
\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(4)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\otimes 3}\Big)
in the Cartesian coordinates, we obtain

\displaystyle\left[\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(4)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\otimes 3}\Big)\right]_{k}=\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{3}=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{4}=1}^{D}T^{(4)}_{k,i_{2},i_{3},i_{4}}\sigma_{i_{2}}\sigma_{i_{3}}\sigma_{i_{4}}

\displaystyle=b_{4}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{3}=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{4}=1}^{D}\sigma_{i_{2}}\sigma_{i_{3}}\sigma_{i_{4}}\Bigg[\Big(B_{ki_{2}}B_{i_{3}i_{4}}+B_{ki_{3}}B_{i_{2}i_{4}}

\displaystyle+B_{ki_{4}}B_{i_{2}i_{3}}\Big)-\frac{2}{(D+4)}\Big[\delta_{ki_{2}}\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]_{i_{3}i_{4}}+\delta_{ki_{3}}\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]_{i_{2}i_{4}}

\displaystyle+\delta_{ki_{4}}\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]_{i_{2}i_{3}}+\delta_{i_{2}i_{3}}\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]_{ki_{4}}+\delta_{i_{2}i_{4}}\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]_{ki_{3}}+\delta_{i_{3}i_{4}}\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]_{ki_{2}}\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{2\mathrm{Tr}\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]}{(D+2)(D+4)}\Big(\delta_{ki_{2}}\delta_{i_{3}i_{4}}+\delta_{ki_{3}}\delta_{i_{2}i_{4}}+\delta_{ki_{4}}\delta_{i_{2}i_{3}}\Big)\Bigg]

(297)

Let us focus on Eq. (
297
) term by term. Firstly,

\displaystyle\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{3}=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{4}=1}^{D}\sigma_{i_{2}}\sigma_{i_{3}}\sigma_{i_{4}}B_{ki_{2}}B_{i_{3}i_{4}}=\bigg[\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{D}B_{ki_{2}}\sigma_{i_{2}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\times\bigg[\sum_{i_{3}=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{4}=1}^{D}\sigma_{i_{3}}B_{i_{3}i_{4}}\sigma_{i_{4}}\bigg]=\big[\mathbf{B}\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\big]_{k}\big(\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\cdot\mathbf{B}\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\big).

(298)

Similarly, summing over the remaining two terms of the form
B_{kb}B_{cd}
, we will also get
\big[\mathbf{B}\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\big]_{k}\big(\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\cdot\mathbf{B}\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\big)
. Focusing on the terms of the form
\delta_{ka}\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]_{cd}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{3}=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{4}=1}^{D}\sigma_{i_{2}}\sigma_{i_{3}}\sigma_{i_{4}}\delta_{ki_{2}}\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]_{i_{3}i_{4}}=\big[\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\big]_{k}\big(\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\cdot\mathbf{B}^{2}\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\big).

(299)

Similarly, summing over the remaining two terms of the form
\delta_{ka}\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]_{cd}
, we will also get
\big[\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\big]_{k}\big(\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\cdot\mathbf{B}^{2}\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\big)
. Focusing on the terms of the form
\delta_{ab}\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]_{kc}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{3}=1}^{D}\sum_{i_{4}=1}^{D}\sigma_{i_{2}}\sigma_{i_{3}}\sigma_{i_{4}}\delta_{i_{2}i_{3}}\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]_{ki_{4}}

\displaystyle=\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\big]_{k}\big(\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\cdot\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\big)

\displaystyle=\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\big]_{k}.

(300)

Similarly, summing over the remaining two terms of the form
\delta_{ab}\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]_{kc}
, we will also get
\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\big]_{k}
. The three remaining terms are of the form
\delta_{ka}\delta_{bc}
. Summing over each of them, we obtain
\big[\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\big]_{k}
. Combining everything and putting it back into Eq. (
297
), we obtain

\displaystyle\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(4)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\otimes 3}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~3b_{4}\bigg[\Big(\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\cdot\mathbf{B}\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\Big)\mathbf{B}\vec{\bm{\sigma}}-\frac{2}{(D+4)}\bigg[\Big(\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\cdot\mathbf{B}^{2}\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\Big)\vec{\bm{\sigma}}+\mathbf{B}^{2}\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{2\mathrm{Tr}\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]}{(D+2)(D+4)}\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\bigg].

(301)

The expansion of
\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(4)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\otimes 4}\Big)
in terms of
\mathbf{B}
and
\vec{\bm{\sigma}}
is given in Eq. (
116
), which reads as

\displaystyle\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(4)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\otimes 4}\Big)

\displaystyle=3b_{4}\bigg[\Big(\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\cdot\mathbf{B}\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\Big)^{2}-\frac{4}{(D+4)}\Big(\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\cdot\mathbf{B}^{2}\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{2\mathrm{Tr}\big[\mathbf{B}^{2}\big]}{(D+2)(D+4)}\bigg].

(302)

Substituting Eqs. (
301
) and (
302
) back into Eq. (
296
), we finally obtain

\displaystyle\vec{\nabla}_{\mathscr{S}}\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(4)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\otimes 4}\Big)

\displaystyle=12b_{4}\bigg[\Big(\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\cdot\mathbf{B}\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\Big)\mathbf{B}\vec{\bm{\sigma}}+\frac{2}{(D+4)}\Big(\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\cdot\mathbf{B}^{2}\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\Big)\vec{\bm{\sigma}}

\displaystyle-\Big(\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\cdot\mathbf{B}\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\Big)^{2}\vec{\bm{\sigma}}-\frac{2}{(D+4)}\mathbf{B}^{2}\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\bigg].

(303)

Appendix P
Checking of Eqs. (
135
) and (
136
)

Using Eq. (
238
), we may write

\nabla^{2}\Big(\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\cdot\mathbf{B}^{2}\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\Big)=2\sum_{k=1}^{D}\left[\mathbf{B}^{2}\right]_{k,k}=2\mathrm{Tr}\left[\mathbf{B}^{2}\right],

(304)

where
\left[\mathbf{B}^{2}\right]_{k,k}
is the
k
-th diagonal element of the matrix
\mathbf{B}^{2}
. Similarly, from Eq. (
239
), we obtain

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}\Big(\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\cdot\mathbf{B}^{2}\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\Big)=2D\Big(\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\cdot\mathbf{B}^{2}\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\Big)+\nabla^{2}_{\mathscr{S}}\Big(\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\cdot\mathbf{B}^{2}\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\Big).

(305)

Comparing Eqs. (
304
) and (
305
) gives

\nabla^{2}_{\mathscr{S}}\Big(\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\cdot\mathbf{B}^{2}\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\Big)=-2D\bigg[\Big(\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\cdot\mathbf{B}^{2}\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\Big)-\frac{1}{D}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\mathbf{B}^{2}\right]\bigg].

(306)

Noting that
\mathrm{Tr}\left[\mathbf{B}^{2}\right]
is independent of
\vec{\bm{\sigma}}
and therefore
\nabla^{2}_{\mathscr{S}}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\mathbf{B}^{2}\right]=0
, we may rewrite Eq. (
306
) as

\bigg[\nabla^{2}_{\mathscr{S}}+2D\bigg]\bigg[\Big(\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\cdot\mathbf{B}^{2}\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\Big)-\frac{1}{D}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\mathbf{B}^{2}\right]\bigg]=0.

(307)

This immediately gives from the definition (
132
) of the projector operator
\mathscr{P}_{4}
that

\mathscr{P}_{4}\bigg[\Big(\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\cdot\mathbf{B}^{2}\vec{\bm{\sigma}}\Big)-\frac{1}{D}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\mathbf{B}^{2}\right]\bigg]=0.

(308)

Equation (
308
) is the desired Eq. (
135
) of the main text.

From Eq. (
240
), we have

\nabla^{2}_{\mathscr{S}}\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(4)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\otimes 4}\Big)=-4(D+2)\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(n)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\otimes n}\Big).

(309)

Hence, we have

\Big[\nabla^{2}_{\mathscr{S}}+2D\Big]\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(4)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\otimes 4}\Big)=-2(D+4)\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(n)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\otimes n}\Big).

(310)

From the definition of the projector operator
\mathscr{P}_{4}
from Eq. (
132
), we may write

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}_{4}\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(4)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\otimes 4}\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\nabla^{2}_{\mathscr{S}}\Big[\nabla^{2}_{\mathscr{S}}+2D\Big]}{8(D+2)(D+4)}\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(4)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\otimes 4}\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4(D+2)}\nabla^{2}_{\mathscr{S}}\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(4)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\otimes 4}\Big)

\displaystyle=\Big(\mathbb{T}^{(4)},\vec{\bm{\sigma}}^{\otimes 4}\Big).

(311)

Equation (
311
) is the desired Eq. (
136
) of the main text.
